# Financial Services — review register

    for:     the consolidated financial-services documents
    started: 2026-10-01
    rule:    every point gets an id here before it is answered (working-with-eric.md §5)

Status is one of `OPEN`, `DECIDED`, `ANSWERED`, `DECLINED` (with reason).
A row does not change status silently.

**Criticality,** for every open row; a closed row has none (FS-779):

- **Critical:** blocks R1's go-live, or exposes us legally, to a regulator
  or to an attack now.
- **High:** blocks a build, a customer commitment, or a decision others
  are waiting on.
- **Medium:** needed later in phase 1, or improves something already
  live.
- **Low:** worth doing some day, or information.

The reader's Review page shows the open rows by criticality first.

| FS-437 | Eric | Can Dibon register with CBK as a fintech or financial-services technology provider? | DECIDED | — | Research running **2026-10-05:** research done. CBK recognises providers only by licence: PSP authorisation under the NPS Act now (its Directory lists 42 PSPs, 23 authorised to provide a platform processing payments for merchants, one for Saccos and informal groups), or a payment gateway licence under the draft Bill (KSh 10m capital). No CBK sandbox today (the Bill would allow one, cl.74); no CBK register of technology providers: draft PG 16 (Sep 2026) has each bank approve material providers, SaaS included, and file a register of them by 5 January. AFIK membership gives a voice, not status. Proposal in chat **2026-10-05:** decided under FS-440 |
| FS-438 | Eric | Can Dibon register as an auditor for institutions that do not use our systems? | DECIDED | — | Research running **2026-10-05:** research done. Statutory financial audits need an ICPAK-licensed firm approved by CBK or SASRA (Banking Act s.24, Microfinance Act ss.29 to 30, Sacco Act ss.44 to 45, Accountants Act s.19): not open to Dibon. Open, with no licence found: penetration tests and IT and cyber assessments (CBK 2017 note, PSP guideline 2019), and independent AML testing (POCAML Regulations regs 11(d), 43). Data protection audits need ODPC accreditation; ISO certification needs KENAS accreditation; critical information infrastructure audits are NC4 staff only. Never for a system we supply, host or operate. Proposal in chat **2026-10-05:** decided under FS-440 |
| FS-439 | Eric | A partnership in which financial institutions offer SME Recon, and perhaps the Odoo business suite, to their SME customers | ANSWERED | — | Proposal in chat **2026-10-05:** proposal in chat. First candidates: KCB and NBK (current customers, CU-03, CU-04) and the banking-as-a-service bank (PT-34) **2026-10-06 (triage, FS-492):** answered by FS-441; FS-442. | FS-672 | Eric | What is the difference between a refund and an M-Pesa reversal? | ANSWERED | — | FS-670. **2026-10-09:** a reversal undoes the original payment by its M-Pesa receipt: Safaricom moves the same amount back to the same wallet, so there is no new payee to check, but only within the window Safaricom allows (rails RL-03, built). A refund is a new payment, a B2C payout to a number, or a bank transfer: a payout like any other, so the registered name is confirmed (RL-21), two people approve (MD-06), it never pays twice (RL-14), and it costs a B2C fee. Proposal in chat: JN-16 §5 asks Safaricom for the reversal window in writing, and a reversal also needs a second person **2026-10-09:** owner: go, all of it. | FS-683 | Eric | What protections can we put in place for the bank payment file? | DECIDED | — | FS-680, FS-682. **2026-10-10:** proposal in chat, in layers: (1) less time exposed: generated on demand, one download by a named person after step-up, logged, deleted after upload; (2) separation: whoever downloads and uploads never releases; (3) a check before release: the portal's view of the uploaded batch exported and uploaded back, compared line by line by the platform, or else by the signatories; (4) the bank's own controls: dual control, limits, name validation on upload, internet banking only from the designated machine, hardware tokens, release alerts; (5) caught within the hour: same-day statement or debit alerts compared, a recall at once on any difference; (6) the real fix, host-to-host files signed and encrypted by the platform, with no person in between. Questions for NCBA and Access Bank **2026-10-10:** owner: go. JN-13 step 4 carries the layers, step 6 the comparison within the hour, 3d the batch deleted and a recall on a line after release; a gap row for the portal's export; the bank questions in §5 |

| FS-755 | Eric | Is there an ITIL definition for service owner, service manager and the like? | DECIDED | — | FS-751. **2026-10-12:** yes, in ITIL 4's glossary: a service owner is accountable for the delivery of one specific service; on the consumer side, a customer sets a service's requirements and owns its outcomes, a user uses it, a sponsor authorises the budget for consuming it; a change authority authorises changes. Service manager is a generic term, and ITIL 4's practice guides add practice owners. Two of our names clash: "Executive sponsor" with ITIL's sponsor, who sits at the customer; our "Service owner", who runs the whole platform, with ITIL's, who answers for one service. Proposal in chat: "Accountable executive"; "Operations lead"; each product owner also the ITIL service owner for that product; the change authority named as such; each institution's customer, user and sponsor recorded in its delivery map |
| FS-756 | Eric | What about roles like security lead, solution architect and compliance: does ITIL define them? | DECIDED | — | FS-755. **2026-10-12:** ITIL 4 names no such job titles; it organises work into practices, each with a practice owner. Our security lead owns information security management; the solution architect, architecture management and service design; the compliance and risk officer, risk management, and is our data protection officer if the Act requires one (s.24, to assess). ISO/IEC 27001 asks only that security roles be assigned, not titled; NIST CSF 2.0 likewise (GV.RR). The names stay. Proposal in chat: a practice-owner column in service management's practices table, mapping every ITIL practice to one of our roles, applied with FS-755's renames |
| FS-757 | Eric | "Accountable executive" is not liked: something more team-oriented | DECIDED | — | FS-755. **2026-10-12:** options in chat: Chair (proposed: chairs the weekly meeting, decides when the team cannot, says yes to what costs money), Team lead, Steward, Convener **2026-10-12:** owner: team lead. Applied with FS-755 and FS-756: team lead (directs, chairs the weekly meeting, decides when the team cannot), operations lead (runs the platform, the change authority for normal changes), each product owner ITIL's service owner for its product; a practice owner for each of the 27 practices in service management §1; Roan Capital's sponsor, customer and users, by role, in its delivery map; the GitLab groups follow |

| FS-762 | Eric | Review Prembly's reseller agreement with Dibon Consulting Kenya and its standard data processing agreement (Kenya) | DECIDED | — | PT-53, SD-11, FS-748. **2026-10-12:** both drafts read; moved from the repository root to sources/confidential/prembly/ (out of git). Against our partner rules (rules §3, C17): a processing agreement is offered, but it casts Dibon as controller, where for borrowers we are the institution's processor and Prembly our sub-processor; breach notice 48 hours in the agreement and 72 in the DPA, which prevails (ours: 24); where data is held not stated, and transfers out of Kenya allowed; sub-processors under general authorisation, unnamed; evidence once a year, a certificate optional; deletion at exit with a certificate in the agreement, on request only in the DPA. Also: no uptime target or response times, support in West African hours; prices changeable on 14 days' notice; prepaid tokens never refunded; suspension on Prembly's opinion of risk; discontinuation told after the fact; no warranty of accuracy; we indemnify, uncapped, for our customers' misuse and any data-law breach; a ban on using results to train predictive models, a breach not curable; arbitration seated in Lagos and insolvency under Nigeria's companies act; liveness and face match missing from the price list; our legal name to be Dibon Company Limited. Recommendation in chat: send our changes before signing; the DPA rewritten for the processor chain; one legal question (CRB and register data resold). **Owner, 2026-10-12: go with all four.** Applied: (1) our changes letter to Prembly, a Word file kept out of git in sources/confidential/prembly/, for the commercial lead to send; (2) PT-53 moved to In discussion, with the C17 assessment; (3) LQ-12, bureau and register data resold; (4) institutions rule 9 and partners rule 9 in docs/rules.md, and the flow-down added to FS-748 **2026-10-12 (triage, FS-779):** applied; the next steps are on partners PT-53 |
